
######## Friday, the 14th of March, 1930 (raining) ########
   ◆ Ada's past: He behaved honestly and steadily, and was never known to miss a payment.
   ◆ Arthur's past: He always considered it a duty to put by something for a rainy day.
   ◆ Mr. Thomas's past: He has never speculated.

=== half past seven in the morning ===
   Ada thinks: She acts so because she has been promised a charwoman's place permanently, and wants to show herself trustworthy.
   Ada [She chooses to empty the ] → Empty the waste-paper baskets and dust the desks.
   ⇒ Ada emptied the baskets and dusted the desks.
   Arthur thinks: He acts so because he is interested in politics.
   Arthur [He chooses to read the mo] → Read the morning paper over breakfast.
   ⇒ Arthur read the paper: A NEW PLANET: the Lowell Observatory in Arizona announces the discovery of a ninth planet beyond Neptune. INDIA: Mr. Gandhi is marching to the sea to make salt in defiance of the Salt Laws.

=== half past eight in the morning ===
   (Mr. Thomas sets Arthur to the quarterly ledger.)
   ⚑ Mr. Thomas: It is Friday, pay-day. Mrs. Turner, the charwoman, is here; her week's wages of twelve shillings are due, and she needs them for her rent. Does Mr. Walter Thomas pay Mrs. Turner her wages now? → YES (3:0) He pays her now, because he has no choice; he must.
   Ada thinks: She acts so because she is proud, and does not wish to be thought a beggar.
   Ada [She chooses to scrub the ] → Scrub the floor under the clerks' desks.
   ⇒ Ada scrubbed the floor under the clerks' desks. Under Mr. Hale's desk she found a half-crown (2s. 6d.) wedged in a crack of the boards.
      Mr. Arthur Hale: "Good morning, Mr. Thomas."
   Arthur thinks: He acts so because he likes her society.
   Arthur [Arthur chooses to pass th] → Pass the time of day with Mrs. Ada Turner.
      Mr. Arthur Hale: "A new planet has been discovered beyond Neptune.  Mr. Gandhi is in India."
      → Arthur now thinks well of Mrs. Ada Turner.
      → Ada now thinks well of Mr. Arthur Hale.
   ⇒ Arthur spoke with Mrs. Ada Turner about the news in the paper.
   Mr. Thomas thinks: He acts so because Mr. Hale is a junior clerk whom he likes.
   Mr. Thomas [He chooses to pass the ti] → Pass the time of day with Mr. Arthur Hale.
      Mr. Walter Thomas: "Set the totals right, and bring the book to me."
      Mr. Arthur Hale: "Half past eight in the morning, Friday, the 14th of March, 1930; raining."
      → Arthur now thinks well of Mr. Walter Thomas.
   ⇒ Mr. Thomas spoke with Mr. Arthur Hale about the work in hand.

=== half past twelve in the afternoon ===

   ⇒ Arthur had soup and a chop for 1s. 2d.
   Mr. Thomas thinks: He chooses to take a chop and a glass of water.
   Mr. Thomas [He chooses to take a chop] → Take his usual chop and a glass of water.
   ⇒ Mr. Thomas lunched.

   ⇒ Mr. Thomas attended to the correspondence.
   Ada thinks: She acts so because she can get good value for her money, and wishes to be thrifty.
   Ada [She chooses to buy a rabb | She chooses to buy bread, | She chooses to buy a rabb votes [0,0,1,2,0]] → Buy a rabbit, onions and a loaf, for a stew (1s. 9d.); she would have 14s. 0d. left.
   ⇒ Ada bought a rabbit, onions and a loaf, for a stew for 1s. 9d.

=== seven o'clock in the evening ===
   Arthur thinks: He acts so because he wants to keep £1 13s.
   Arthur [He chooses to give Edith  | — | Arthur may: votes [0,0,1,0,0,0,0]] → Give Edith 40 shillings for the week's housekeeping; he would keep £1 13s. 3d..
   ⇒ Arthur gave Edith 40s. for the housekeeping.
   Ada thinks: Ada may: (A) Pawn her late husband's watch for ten shillings, to pay the rent.
   Ada [— | Ada may: (A) Pawn her lat | She chooses to cook the s votes [0,0,1,1,0]] → Pawn her late husband's watch for ten shillings, to pay the rent.

--- Mrs. Ada Turner's diary, Friday, the 14th of March, 1930 (3/4 key facts kept) ---
To-day I received twelve shillings wages, found half-a-crown, spoke to Mr. Arthur Hale about the news, pawned my husband's watch for ten shillings, and Bert gave me 3s. 6d. rent.

--- Mr. Arthur Hale's diary, Friday, the 14th of March, 1930 (3/4 key facts kept) ---
To-day I balanced the ledger, spoke to Mrs. Turner and Mr. Thomas about the news, was paid my wages, and entered the total in the ledger.

--- Mr. Walter Thomas's diary, Friday, the 14th of March, 1930 (2/2 key facts kept) ---
To-day I balanced the quarterly ledger, and paid Mrs. Turner her week's wages. I spoke to Mr. Arthur Hale about the work in hand.

--- Mrs. Ada Turner's diary, Tuesday, the 1st of April, 1930 (0/1 key facts kept) ---
To-day I spoke with Mr. Walter Thomas, about the work in hand, and he told me to call to-morrow morning at ten.

--- Mr. Arthur Hale's diary, Tuesday, the 1st of April, 1930 (1/2 key facts kept) ---
To-day I began to earn an increased salary of five shillings a week, and spoke to Mr. Walter Thomas about the work in hand; and I spoke to Joan about what she did to-day.

--- Mr. Walter Thomas's diary, Tuesday, the 1st of April, 1930 (0/0 key facts kept) ---
To-day I spoke with Mrs. Ada Turner about the work in hand, and she seemed pleased to hear that it was going on so well. Afterwards I spoke with Mr. Arthur Hale on the same subject, and he too expressed himself satisfied.

   ◇ Arthur reflects: He thinks her thrifty and neat, because she has made the 40 shillings stretch over the week without running into debt.
   ◇ Mr. Thomas reflects: He thinks him worthy, because he contributed to a charity, walked with Joan in the park, and behaved himself respectfully.
   ◇ Mr. Thomas reflects: He thinks her a respectable, hard-working, and honest woman, because she was punctual and diligent in the performance of her duties, and never gave him any trouble.

--- Mrs. Ada Turner's diary, Monday, the 21st of April, 1930 (1/1 key facts kept) ---
To-day Bert and I worked at the docks from eight in the morning till six at night. We began unloading grain from a Canadian ship, and finished clearing the holds of a French steamer. It was hot, dusty work, and Bert had a bad headache all day. In the evening he felt so ill that he could eat no supper, and went early to bed. I was very tired too, and am sorry to have spent a unprofitable day.

--- Mr. Arthur Hale's diary, Monday, the 21st of April, 1930 (0/0 key facts kept) ---
Monday, 21st April, 1930. To-day I spoke with Edith about how the day had gone, and was glad to hear that she and Joan had been well.

--- Mrs. Ada Turner's diary, Wednesday, the 30th of April, 1930 (2/2 key facts kept) ---
To-day I spoke with Mr. Walter Thomas about the work in hand, and he said he would call for me to-morrow morning at nine o'clock. I spoke also to Bert about how the day went at the docks, and he said things were dull. I asked the landlord to wait for the rent, and he grumbled, but said he would wait till Monday. So I resolved never to ask a favour of a landlord again.

--- Mr. Arthur Hale's diary, Wednesday, the 30th of April, 1930 (2/2 key facts kept) ---
To-day I spoke with Joan about what she did, and with Mr. Walter Thomas about the work in hand; and I felt a little anxious about both.

--- Mr. Walter Thomas's diary, Wednesday, the 30th of April, 1930 (0/0 key facts kept) ---
I spoke to-day with Mrs. Ada Turner about the work in hand, and to Mr. Arthur Hale about the work in hand; stern, exact, dutiful, fair and secretly kind.

--- Mrs. Ada Turner's diary, Friday, the 23rd of May, 1930 (1/2 key facts kept) ---
To-day I received twelve shillings from Mr. Walter Thomas, and spoke to him and Mr. Arthur Hale about the work in hand. The weather is fine, and things are well at home. Bert gave me 3s. 6d. towards the rent.

--- Mr. Arthur Hale's diary, Friday, the 23rd of May, 1930 (2/2 key facts kept) ---
Friday, May 23rd, 1930. To-day I spoke with Mrs. Edith Hale about how the day has gone, and I spoke with Mrs. Ada Turner about the weather and how things are at home, and I spoke with Mr. Walter Thomas about the work in hand. I was paid my week's wages, and I gave Mrs. Edith Hale forty-five shillings for the week's housekeeping.

--- Mr. Walter Thomas's diary, Friday, the 23rd of May, 1930 (0/1 key facts kept) ---
null

--- Mrs. Ada Turner's diary, Sunday, the 25th of May, 1930 (1/2 key facts kept) ---

--- Mr. Arthur Hale's diary, Sunday, the 25th of May, 1930 (0/1 key facts kept) ---
Mr. Arthur Hale, 34, ledger clerk at an insurance office in Moorgate, £3 10s. a week; wife Edith, daughter Joan (7). Conscientious, careful with money, timid with superiors, and glad to be at home of an evening.

--- Mrs. Ada Turner's diary, Friday, the 30th of May, 1930 (1/2 key facts kept) ---
To-day I received twelve shillings from Mr. Walter Thomas, and spoke to him about the work in hand. I also spoke to Mr. Arthur Hale about the weather and how things are at home, and I had further speech with Mr. Walter Thomas about the work in hand. Bert gave me half of the rent, 3s. 6d.

--- Mr. Arthur Hale's diary, Friday, the 30th of May, 1930 (3/3 key facts kept) ---
To-day I spoke to Joan about what she did; to Mrs. Turner about the weather and how things are at home; to Mr. Thomas about the work in hand. I was paid my wages, and gave Edith forty shillings for the week's housekeeping.

--- Mr. Walter Thomas's diary, Friday, the 30th of May, 1930 (1/1 key facts kept) ---
To-day I paid Mrs. Turner her week's wages, and spoke to her and to Mr. Hale about the work in hand.

--- Mrs. Ada Turner's diary, Friday, the 6th of June, 1930 (2/2 key facts kept) ---
To-day Mr. Walter Thomas paid me my week's wages, twelve shillings. He has recommended Mr. Arthur Hale to succeed him as chief clerk. I spoke to Mr. Hale about the weather and how things are at home. Bert gave me his half of the rent, 3s. 6d.

   Arthur thinks: He acts so because he is anxious to complete them as soon as possible.
   Arthur [He chooses to get on with] → Get on with the day's ordinary entries in the books.
   ⇒ Arthur worked at the day's entries.
   Mr. Thomas thinks: He acts so because he has a great deal of writing to get through, and because Mr. Hale has.
   Mr. Thomas [He chooses to attend to t] → Attend to the correspondence at his own desk.
   ⇒ Mr. Thomas attended to the correspondence.
   Ada thinks: She acts so because she has only 6d.
   Ada [She chooses to buy bread, | She chooses to buy scrag- | She chooses to buy a rabb votes [1,0,1,1,0]] → Buy scrag-end of mutton, potatoes and a cabbage (1s. 4d.); she would have £7 11s. 9d. left.
   ⇒ Ada bought scrag-end of mutton, potatoes and a cabbage for 1s. 4d.

=== seven o'clock in the evening ===
   Arthur thinks: He chooses to talk with Mrs. Hale about how the day has gone.
   Arthur [He chooses to talk with M] → Talk with Mrs. Edith Hale about how the day has gone.
   Ada thinks: Seven o'clock in the evening, Wednesday, the 11th of June, 1930; hot.
   Ada [Seven o'clock in the even] → Talk with Bert Turner about how the day went at the docks.

   ⇒ Arthur spoke with Mrs. Edith Hale about how the day has gone.
